for all
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n-1})\in U
. Then the following hold:

(a)

(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{1}=\ldots=(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n-1}=0\iff\nabla\varphi(0)=0

(b)

If
\nabla\varphi(0)=0
and
(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}>0
, then

\text{$g$ is strictly quasiconcave at $x$}\iff\hess\varphi(0)\succ 0.

Proof.

Taking the derivative of
(*)
with respect to
\xi_{i}
, we get

(**)\qquad(\nabla g(x+\xi_{1}v_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n-1}v_{n-1}+\varphi(\xi)v_{n}))^{T}\left(v_{i}+\frac{\partial\varphi(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{i}}v_{n}\right)=0

for all
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
\xi\in U
. Setting here
\xi
to
0
,
we get

(\nabla g(x))^{T}\left(v_{i}+\frac{\partial\varphi(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{i}}\middle|_{\xi=0}v_{n}\right)=0

for each
i\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
. From this, (a) follows easily (for “
{\implies}
” use that
(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}\neq 0
since
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
is a basis).
Taking the derivative of
(**)
with respect to
\xi_{j}
, we get

\left(v_{j}+\frac{\partial\varphi(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{j}}v_{n}\right)^{T}(\hess g(x+\xi_{1}v_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n-1}v_{n-1}+\varphi(\xi)v_{n}))\left(v_{i}+\frac{\partial\varphi(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{i}}v_{n}\right)\\
+(\nabla g(x+\xi_{1}v_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n-1}v_{n-1}+\varphi(\xi)v_{n}))^{T}\left(\frac{\partial^{2}\varphi(\xi)}{\partial\xi_{i}\partial\xi_{j}}v_{n}\right)=0

for all
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
and
\xi\in U
.
To prove (b), suppose now that
\nabla\varphi(0)=0
and
(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}>0
. Then the preceding equation implies

\hess\varphi(0)=-\frac{1}{(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}}(v_{i}^{T}(\hess g(x))v_{j})_{i,j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}}.

Since
v_{1},\ldots,v_{n-1}
now form a basis of the orthogonal complement of
\nabla g(x)
by (a), the matrix

(v_{i}^{T}(\hess g(x))v_{j})_{i,j\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}}
is negative definite if and only if
g
is strictly quasiconcave at
x

(see Definition
2.1
).
∎

The following proposition is important for understanding the notion of quasiconcavity. It is trivial that
quasiconcavity of a polynomial
g
at
x
depends only on
x
and the function
V\to\mathbb{R},\ x\mapsto g(x)

where
V
is an arbitrarily small neighborhood of
x
. But if
g(x)=0
and

\nabla g(x)\neq 0
, then it actually
depends only on
x
and the function

V\to\{-1,0,1\},\ x\mapsto\sgn(g(x))

as the equivalence of Conditions (a) and (b) of the following proposition show.

Notation 2.4
.

For
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
, we call

Z(g):=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mid g(x)=0\}

the
real zero set
of
g
.

Proposition 2.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that

g(x)=0\text{ and }\nabla g(x)\neq 0.

Suppose that
V
is a neighborhood of
x
. Then the following are equivalent:

(a)

g
is strictly quasiconcave at
x
.

(b)

There is a basis
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, an open neighborhood
U
of
0
in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}

and a smooth function
\varphi\colon U\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\varphi(0)=0
,
\nabla\varphi(0)=0
,

\hess\varphi(0)\succ 0
,

(*)\qquad x+\xi_{1}v_{1}+\ldots+\xi_{n-1}v_{n-1}+\varphi(\xi)v_{n}\in Z(g)\cap V

for all
\xi\in U
and

(**)\qquad x+\lambda v_{n}\in S(g)\cap V

for all small enough
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
.

(c)

Condition (b) holds with “basis” replaced by “orthogonal basis”.

For any basis
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
like in (b), one has

(***)\qquad(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{1}=\ldots=(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n-1}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}>0.

Proof.

Using Lemma
2.3
(a), it is easy to show that any
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
like in (b) satisfy
(***)
using that
(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}=0
would
contradict the hypothesis
\nabla g(x)\neq 0
since
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
is a basis. Now Part (b) of the same lemma shows that (b) implies (a).
Since it is trivial that (c) implies (b), it only remains to show that (a) implies (c).

To this end,
let (a) be satisfied. In order to show (c), choose an orthogonal basis
v_{1},\dots,v_{n}
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
satisfying
(***)
.
The implicit function theorem yields an open neighborhood

U
of the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
such that for each
\xi=(\xi_{1},\dots,\xi_{n-1})\in U
there is a
unique
\varphi(\xi)\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
(*)
, in particular
\varphi(0)=0
. Moreover, one can choose
U
such that the resulting function

\varphi\colon U\to\mathbb{R}
is smooth. From
(\nabla g(x))^{T}v_{n}>0
, we get
(**)
. From Part (a) of Lemma
2.3
, we get

\nabla\varphi(0)=0
. From Part (b) of the same lemma and from (a), we obtain
\hess\varphi(0)\succ 0
.
∎

Remark 2.6
.

For
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the following are obviously equivalent:

(a)

g
is strictly quasiconcave at
x
.

(b)

\exists\lambda\in\mathbb{R}:\lambda(\nabla g(x))(\nabla g(x))^{T}\succ(\hess g)(x)

Proposition 2.7
.

\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\succeq 0}:=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}\mid A\succeq 0\}
is a cone in the vector space

S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
whose interior
[
\to

4.18
]
is

\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\succ 0}:=\{A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}\mid A\succ 0\}
.

Proof.

Equip
S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with the norm defined by

\|A\|:=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\\
\|x\|\leq 1\end{subarray}}|x^{T}Ax|

for
A\in S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
. By
2.2
(c), this norm (as any other norm)
induces the unique vector space topology on
S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
.

If
A
is an interior point of
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\succeq 0}
, then there exists
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that

A-\varepsilon I_{n}\succeq 0
and thus
A\succeq\varepsilon I_{n}\succ 0
.

Conversely, let
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
satisfy

A\succ 0
. We show that
A
is an interior point of
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\succeq 0}
.
By
3.3
, the lowest eigenvalue
\varepsilon
of
A
is nonnegative since
A\succeq 0
. Actually, we have
even
\varepsilon>0

since
A
has trivial kernel due to
A\succ 0
. Now
A-\varepsilon I_{n}
has only nonnegative
eigenvalues and thus
A-\varepsilon I_{n}\succeq 0
by
3.3
.
It suffices to show that a ball around
A
with radius
\varepsilon
in

S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
is contained in
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\succeq 0}
. For this purpose, let

B\in S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
with
\|B-A\|\leq\varepsilon
and fix
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
\|x\|=1
. We have to show that

x^{T}Bx\geq 0
. But we have
x^{T}Bx=x^{T}Ax+x^{T}(B-A)x\geq x^{T}Ax-\|B-A\|\geq\varepsilon x^{T}I_{n}x-\varepsilon=0
.
∎

Lemma 2.8
.

Let
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
. If
g
is strictly (quasi-)concave at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
then there is a neighborhood
U
of
x
such that
g
is
strictly (quasi-)concave on
U
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the openness of
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}_{\succ 0}
[
\to

2.7
]
by the continuity of
\mathbb{R}^{n}\to S\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\ x\mapsto(\hess g)(x)
. The second statement follows
similarly by using the equivalence of (a) and (b) in Remark
2.6
.
∎

Remark 2.9
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
g(x)=0
. Then

\displaystyle(\nabla(g(1-g)^{k}))(x)

\displaystyle=(\nabla g)(x)\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle(\hess(g(1-g)^{k}))(x)

\displaystyle=(\hess g-2k(\nabla g)(\nabla g)^{T})(x).

Lemma 2.10
.

Suppose
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
,
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
g(u)=0
.
Then the following are equivalent:

(a)

g
is strictly quasiconcave at
u
.

(b)

There exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
g(1-g)^{k}
is strictly concave at
u
.

(c)

There exists
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
with
\ell\geq k
, we have
that
g(1-g)^{\ell}
is strictly concave at
u
.

Proof.

Combine Remarks
2.9
and
2.6
.
∎

3
Lagrange multipliers from real closed fields

Remark 3.1
.

If
u,x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
with
v:=x-u\neq 0
,
g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
,
g
is strictly quasiconcave at
u
,
g(u)=0
and
g\geq 0
on
\conv\{u,x\}
, then
obviously
(\nabla g(u))^{T}v>0
.

Lemma 3.2

(Existence of real Lagrange multipliers)
.

Suppose
u\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
f\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
.
Let
U
be a neighborhood of
u
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
U\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

is convex and not a singleton. Moreover, suppose
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}
are strictly quasiconcave at
u
,
f\geq 0
on
U\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
and

f(u)=g_{1}(u)=\ldots=g_{m}(u)=0.

Then there are
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{m}\in\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

\nabla f(u)=\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}\nabla g_{i}(u).

Proof.

Choose
x\in U\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
with
v:=x-u\neq 0
. By Remark
3.1
, we have

(\nabla g_{i}(u))^{T}v>0

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
since

U\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is convex. Assume the required Lagrange multipliers do not exist. Then
4.20
yields
w\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

such that
((\nabla f)(u))^{T}w<0
and
((\nabla g_{i})(u))^{T}w\geq 0
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Replacing
w
by
w+\varepsilon v
for some
small
\varepsilon>0
, we get even
((\nabla g_{i})(u))^{T}w>0
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Then for all sufficiently small
\delta\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
,
we have
u+\delta v\in U\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
but
f(u+\delta v)<0

\lightning
.
∎

Definition 3.3
.

[
\to

2.5
]
Let
M
be a topological space and
A\subseteq M
.
We call

\partial A:=\overline{A}\setminus A^{\circ}=\overline{A}\cap\overline{M\setminus A}

the
boundary
of
A
.

Definition 3.4
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
We call

\convbd S:=S\cap\partial\conv S

the
convex boundary
of
S
.
Obviously,

\convbd S=\{x\in S\mid\forall U\in\mathscr{U}_{x}:U\not\subseteq\conv S\}.

We say that
S

has nonempty interior near its convex boundary

if
\convbd S\subseteq\overline{S^{\circ}}
.

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
S\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then

\convbd S=\{u\in S\mid\exists\varphi\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{*}\setminus\{0\}:\forall x\in S:\varphi(u)\leq\varphi(x)\}.

Proof.

“
\supseteq
” Let
u\in S
and
\varphi\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{*}\setminus\{0\}
such that

\forall x\in S:\varphi(u)\leq\varphi(x)
. Then even
\forall x\in\conv S:\varphi(u)\leq\varphi(x)
. Choose

v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
\varphi(v)>0
. Then
\varphi(u-\varepsilon v)<\varphi(u)
and hence
u-\varepsilon v\notin\conv S
for
each
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
. It follows that no neighborhood of
u
is contained in the convex hull of

S
. Hence
u\in\convbd S
.

“
\subseteq
”
If
\dim\conv S<n
[
\to

4.7
], we have
\partial\conv S=\overline{\conv S}
and hence

\convbd S=S
and one easily finds
\varphi\in(\mathbb{R}^{n})^{*}\setminus\{0\}
that is constant on

\conv S
. So now suppose that
\dim\conv S=n
. Let
u\in\convbd S
. By
Theorem
4.17
, we get an exposed face
F
of
\conv S
with
\dim F<n
and
u\in F
.
Choose
\varphi\colon\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
linear such that

F=\{y\in\conv S\mid\forall x\in\conv S:\varphi(y)\leq\varphi(x)\}.

Since
\dim F<n
, we have obviously
\varphi\neq 0
.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
B\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a closed ball in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
.
Suppose that
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
are strictly quasiconcave on
B
.
Then the following hold:

(a)

S:=B\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is convex.

(b)

Every linear form from
\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]\setminus\{0\}
[
\to

6.1
(a)] has at most one minimizer on
S
.

(c)

Let
u
be a minimizer of the linear form
f\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]\setminus\{0\}
on
S
and set

I:=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}\mid g_{i}(u)=0\}.

Then
u

is also minimizer of
f
on
S^{\prime}:=\{x\in B\mid\forall i\in I:g_{i}(x)\geq 0\}\supseteq S
.

Proof.

(a)
Let
x,y\in S
with
x\neq y
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. The polynomial

f:=g_{i}(Tx+(1-T)y)\in\mathbb{R}[T]

attains a minimum
a
on
[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}
[
\to

1.19
]. We have to show
a\geq 0
.
Because of
f(0)=g_{i}(y)\geq 0
and
f(1)=g_{i}(x)\geq 0
, it is enough to show that this minimum is not attained
in a point
t\in(0,1)_{\mathbb{R}}
. Assume it is. Then
f^{\prime}(t)=0
, i.e.,
((\nabla g_{i})(z))^{T}v=0
for
z:=tx+(1-t)y

and
v:=x-y\neq 0
. Since
z\in B
and hence
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave at
z
, it follows that

v^{T}((\hess g_{i})(z))v<0
, i.e.,
f^{\prime\prime}(t)<0
. Then
f<a
on a neighborhood of
t
[
\to

5.3
(b)]

\lightning
.

(b) Suppose
x
and
y
are minimizers of the linear form
f\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]\setminus\{0\}
on
S
.
Then
x,y\in\convbd S
by
3.5
. Since
f
is linear, it is constant on
\aff\{x,y\}
. Hence even

\conv\{x,y\}\overset{\text{(a)}}{\subseteq}\aff\{x,y\}\cap S\overset{\ref{convbdchar}}{\subseteq}\convbd S\overset{\text{(a)}}{=}S\cap\partial S=S\cap(\overline{S}\setminus S^{\circ})\overset{\text{$S$ closed}}{=}S\setminus S^{\circ}=\partial S.

Since
\conv\{x,y\}\setminus\{x,y\}\subseteq B^{\circ}
, we have then that

\conv\{x,y\}\setminus\{x,y\}\subseteq Z(g_{1}\dotsm g_{m})
.
Assume now for a contradiction that
x\neq y
.
Then this implies that at least one of the
g_{i}
vanishes on
\aff\{x,y\}
. Fix a corresponding
i
.
Setting
v:=y-x
, we have then
((\nabla g_{i})(x))^{T}v=0
and
v^{T}((\hess g_{i})(x))v=0
.
Since
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave at
x
, this implies
v=0
, i.e.,
x=y
as desired.

(c) By definition of
I
, the sets
S
and
S^{\prime}
coincide on a neighborhood of
u
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Hence
u
is a

local
minimizer of
f
on
S^{\prime}
. Since
S^{\prime}
is convex by (a) and
f
is linear,
u
is also a (
global
)
minimizer of
f
on
S^{\prime}
.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Suppose
B
is a closed ball in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
.
Suppose that
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
are strictly quasiconcave on
B
and that
S:=B\cap S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

has nonempty interior. Then the following hold:

(a)

For every real closed extension field
R
of
\mathbb{R}

and all linear forms
f\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]\setminus\{0\}
,

f
has a unique minimizer on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)
.

(b)

For every real closed extension field
R
of
\mathbb{R}
,
all linear forms
f\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
with

\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1

[
\to

1.10
]
(note that
\nabla f\in R^{n}
as
f
is linear) and every

u\in\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(B^{\circ})
which minimizes
f
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)
,
there are
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{m}\in\mathscr{O}_{R}\cap R_{\geq 0}
with

\lambda_{1}+\ldots+\lambda_{m}\notin\mathfrak{m}_{R}

such that both
f-f(u)-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{i}g_{i}
and its gradient
vanish at
u
.

Proof.

(a) Consider the class of all real closed extension fields
R
of
\mathbb{R}
such that
all linear forms from
R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]\setminus\{0\}
have a unique minimizer on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)
.
By real quantifier elimination [
\to

8.17
], this is easily seen to be a
0
-ary
\mathbb{R}
-semialgebraic class
[
\to

8.3
]. By
8.5
, this class is either empty or consists of all
real closed extensions fields of
\mathbb{R}
. Hence it suffices to prove the statement in the case
R=\mathbb{R}

[
\to

8.19
]. But then the unicity part follows from Lemma
3.6
(b) and the existence part
from
1.19
.

(b) Now let
R
be a real closed field extension of
\mathbb{R}
,
f\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
a linear form with
\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1
and

u
a minimizer of
f
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S\cap B^{\circ})
.
Set

I:=\{i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}\mid g_{i}(u)=0\}

and define the set

S^{\prime}:=\{x\in B\mid\forall i\in I:g_{i}(x)\geq 0\}\supseteq S

which is convex by
3.6
(a).
Using the Tarski principle [
\to

8.19
],
one shows easily that
u
is a minimizer of
f
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S^{\prime})
by Lemma
3.6
(c).
Note also that of course
u\in\mathscr{O}_{R}^{n}
and
\st(u)\in S
.

Now Lemma
3.2
says in particular that for all linear forms
\widetilde{f}\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
minimizers
\widetilde{u}
of
\widetilde{f}
on
S^{\prime}\cap B^{\circ}
with
\forall i\in I:g_{i}(\widetilde{u})=0
, there is a family

(\lambda_{i})_{i\in I}
in
\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
such that

\nabla\widetilde{f}=\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\nabla g_{i}(\widetilde{u}).

Using the Tarski principle [
\to

8.19
], we see that actually for all real closed extension fields

\widetilde{R}
of
\mathbb{R}
, all linear forms
\widetilde{f}\in\widetilde{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
all minimizers
\widetilde{u}
of
\widetilde{f}
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S^{\prime}\cap B^{\circ})

with
\forall i\in I:g_{i}(\widetilde{u})=0
, there is a family

(\lambda_{i})_{i\in I}
in
R_{\geq 0}
such that

\nabla\widetilde{f}=\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\nabla g_{i}(\widetilde{u}).

We apply this to
\widetilde{R}:=R
,
\widetilde{u}:=u
,
\widetilde{f}:=f
and thus obtain a family

(\lambda_{i})_{i\in I}
in
R_{\geq 0}
such that

(*)\qquad\nabla f=\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\nabla g_{i}(u).

In order to show that
\lambda_{i}\in\mathscr{O}_{R}
for all
i\in I
, we choose a point
x\in S^{\prime\circ}\neq\emptyset
with
\prod_{i\in I}g_{i}(x)\neq 0

and thus
g_{i}(x)>0
for all
i\in I
. Setting
v:=x-u\in\mathscr{O}_{R}^{n}
, we get from
(*)
that

(\nabla f)^{T}v=\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}(\nabla g_{i}(u))^{T}v
. Since
\st(u)\in S^{\prime}
and
S^{\prime}
is convex,
Remark
3.1
yields
\st((\nabla g_{i}(u))^{T}v)=(\nabla g_{i}(\st(u)))^{T}\st(v)>0
for all
i\in I

(use that
\st(u)\neq x
since
g_{i}(\st(u))=0
while
g_{i}(x)>0
).
Together with
\lambda_{i}\geq 0
for all
i\in I
, this shows
\lambda_{i}\in\mathscr{O}_{R}
for all
i\in I
as desired.

It now suffices to show that
\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\notin\mathfrak{m}_{R}
. But this is clear since
(*)
yields in particular

1=\|\nabla f\|_{2}\leq\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\|(\nabla g_{i})(u)\|_{2}\leq\left(\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\right)\max_{i\in I}\|(\nabla g_{i})(u)\|_{2}

(note that
I\neq\emptyset
by the first inequality) which readily implies
\sum_{i\in I}\lambda_{i}\notin\mathfrak{m}_{R}
.
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
such that
S:=S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is compact and has nonempty interior near its convex boundary.
Suppose that
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave on

(\convbd S)\cap Z(g_{i})

for each

i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
.
Let
R
be real closed extension field of
\mathbb{R}
and

f\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
be a linear form with
\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1
.
Then the following hold:

(a)

F:=\{u\in S\mid\forall x\in S:\st(f(u))\leq\st(f(x))\}

is a finite subset of
\convbd S
.

(b)

S^{\prime}:=\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)\subseteq\mathscr{O}_{R}^{n}
and

f
has a unique minimizer
x_{u}
on

\{x\in S^{\prime}\mid\st(x)=u\}

for each
u\in F
.

(c)

For every
u\in F
, there are

\lambda_{u1},\ldots,\lambda_{um}\in\mathscr{O}_{R}\cap R_{\geq 0}
with

\lambda_{u1}+\ldots+\lambda_{um}\notin\mathfrak{m}_{R}

such that both
f-f(x_{u})-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\lambda_{ui}g_{i}
and its gradient
vanish at
x_{u}
.

Proof.

(a) Obviously
\st(f)\neq 0
and hence

F=\{u\in S\mid\forall x\in S:(\st(f))(u)\leq(\st(f))(x)\}\subseteq\convbd S

by Proposition
3.5
.
We now prove that
F
is finite.
WLOG
S\neq\emptyset
. Set [
\to

1.19
]

a:=\min\{(\st(f))(x)\mid x\in S\}

so that

F=\{u\in S\mid(\st(f))(u)=a\}.

By compactness of
S
, it is enough to show that
every
x\in S
possesses a neighborhood
U
in
S
such that
U\cap F\subseteq\{x\}
.
This is trivial for the points of
S\setminus F
. So consider an arbitrary point
x\in F
.
Since
x\in\convbd S
, each
g_{i}
is positive or strictly
quasiconcave at
x
. According to
2.8
, we can choose a closed ball
B
of positive radius around

x
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that each
g_{i}
is positive or strictly
quasiconcave even on
B
. By Lemma
3.6
(b),
\st(f)
has at most one minimizer on
U:=S\cap B
,
namely
x
, i.e.,
U\cap F\subseteq\{x\}
.

(b) First observe that

S^{\prime}:=\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)\subseteq\mathscr{O}_{R}^{n}
since the
transfer from
\mathbb{R}
to
R
is an isomorphism of Boolean algebras [
\to

9.5
]:
Choosing
N\in\mathbb{N}
with
S\subseteq[-N,N]_{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we have
S^{\prime}\subseteq\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}([-N,N]^{n}_{\mathbb{R}})=[-N,N]^{n}_{R}\subseteq\mathscr{O}_{R}^{n}
.

Now we fix
u\in F
and we show that
f
has a unique minimizer on

A:=\{x\in S^{\prime}\mid\st(x)=u\}.

Choose
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
each
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave or positive on
the ball

B:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mid\|v-u\|\leq\varepsilon\}.

Since
u\in\convbd S\subseteq\overline{S^{\circ}}
,
Lemma
3.7
(a) says that
f
has a unique minimizer
x
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S\cap B)
.
Because of
A\subseteq\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S\cap B)
, it is thus enough to show
x\in A
.
Note that
u\in F\cap B\subseteq S\cap B\subseteq\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S\cap B)
and thus

f(x)\leq f(u)
. This implies
\st(f(\st(x)))=\st(f(x))\leq\st(f(u))
which yields together with
\st(x)\in S
that

\st(x)\in F
(and
\st(f(\st(x)))=\st(f(u))
). Again by Lemma
3.7
(a),
\st(f)
has a unique minimizer on

S\cap B
. But
u
and
\st(x)
are both a minimizer of
\st(f)
on
S\cap B
(note that
\st(x)\in S\cap B
).
Hence
u=\st(x)
and thus
x\in A
as desired.

(c) Fix
u\in F
. Choose again
\varepsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that
each
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave or positive on
the ball
B:=\{v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mid\|v-u\|\leq\varepsilon\}
and such that
B\cap F=\{u\}
.
Since
x_{u}\in\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(B^{\circ})
obviously minimizes
f
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S\cap B)
, we get the necessary
Lagrange multipliers by Lemma
3.7
(b).
∎

4
Linear polynomials and truncated quadratic modules

Exercise 4.1
.

For all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in[0,1]_{\mathbb{R}}
, we have
x(1-x)^{k}\leq\frac{1}{k}
.

The main geometric idea in the proof of the following theorem is as follows: Consider a hyperplane that isolates a basic closed
semialgebraic subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and that is defined over a real closed extension field of
\mathbb{R}
. Because we want to apply
Theorem
1.14
to get a sums of squares “isolation certificate”, the points where the hyperplane gets infinitesimally
close to the set pose problems unless the hyperplane
exactly
touches the set in the respective point. The idea is
to find a nonlinear infinitesimal deformation of the hyperplane so that all “infinitesimally near points” becoming “touching points”.
This would be easier (although still not obvious) if there is at most one “infinitesimally near point” but since we deal in this article
with
not necessarily convex
basic closed semialgebraic sets, it is crucial to cope with several such points.

Theorem 4.2
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
such that
M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is Archimedean and suppose that
S:=S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

has nonempty interior near its convex boundary.
Suppose that
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave on
(\convbd S)\cap Z(g_{i})
for each

i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
. Let
R
be a real closed extension field of
\mathbb{R}
and

\ell\in\mathscr{O}_{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]_{1}
such that
\ell\geq 0
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)
.
Then
\ell
lies in the quadratic module generated by
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}
in
\mathscr{O}_{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
.

Proof.

We will apply Theorem
1.14
. Since
S
is compact, we can rescale the
g_{i}
and suppose WLOG that

g_{i}\leq 1\text{ on }S

for
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
Let
M
denote the quadratic module generated by
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}
in
\mathscr{O}_{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
.
Since
M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is Archimedean, also
M
is Archimedean by

1.13
(b) and
1.2
(b). Moreover,
S
could now alternatively be defined
from
M
as in Theorem
1.14
. Write

\ell=f-c

with a linear form
f\in\mathscr{O}_{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
c\in\mathscr{O}_{R}
.
By a rescaling argument, we can suppose that at least one of the coefficients of
\ell
lies
in
\mathscr{O}_{R}^{\times}
[
\to

4.7
].
If
\st(\ell(x))>0
for all
x\in S
, then Theorem
1.14
applied to
\ell
with
k=0
yields
\ell\in M

and we are done. Hence we can from now on suppose that there is some
u\in S
with
\st(\ell(u))=0
. For such an
u
,
we have
\st(c)=\st(f(u))
so that at least one coefficient of
f
must lie
\mathscr{O}_{R}^{\times}
.
By another rescaling, we now can suppose WLOG that
\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1
. Now we are in the situation of
Lemma
3.8
and we define

F,\quad(x_{u})_{u\in F}\quad\text{and}\quad(\lambda_{ui})_{(u,i)\in F\times\{1,\ldots,m\}}

accordingly. Note that

F=\{u\in S\mid\st(\ell(u))=0\}\neq\emptyset

since
\st(\ell(x))\geq 0
for all
x\in S
.
We have
f(x_{u})-c=\ell(x_{u})\geq 0
and

\st(f(x_{u})-c)=\st(\ell(u))=0

for all
u\in F
.
Hence
f(x_{u})-c\in\mathfrak{m}_{R}\cap R_{\geq 0}
for all
u\in F
. We thus have

\ell-\underbrace{(f(x_{u})-c)}_{=:\lambda_{u0}\in\mathfrak{m}_{R}\cap R_{\geq 0}}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\underbrace{\lambda_{ui}}_{\in\mathscr{O}_{R}\cap R_{\geq 0}}g_{i}\in I_{x_{u}}^{2}

for all
u\in F
by
3.8
(c) and
1.13
. Evaluating this in
x_{u}
(and using
g_{i}(x_{u})\geq 0
) yields

(
*
)

\displaystyle g_{i}(x_{u})\neq 0\implies\lambda_{ui}=0\qquad\text{and thus}

(
**
)

\displaystyle\lambda_{ui}g_{i}\equiv_{I_{x_{u}}^{2}}\lambda_{ui}g_{i}(1-g_{i})^{k}

for all
u\in F
,
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
By the Chinese remainder theorem, we find polynomials
s_{0},\ldots,s_{m}\in\mathscr{O}_{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]

such that
s_{i}\equiv_{I_{x_{u}}^{3}}\sqrt{\lambda_{ui}}\in\mathscr{O}_{R}
for all
u\in F
and
i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}

because the ideals
I_{x_{u}}^{3}
(
u\in F
) are pairwise coprime [
\to

1.7
] (use that

\st(x_{u})=u\neq v=\st(x_{v})
for all
u,v\in F
with
u\neq v
).
By an easy scaling argument, we can even guarantee that the coefficients
of
s_{0}
lie in
\mathfrak{m}_{R}
since
\sqrt{\lambda_{u0}}\in\mathfrak{m}_{R}
. Then we have

(
***
)

s_{i}^{2}\equiv_{I_{x_{u}}^{3}}\lambda_{ui}

which means in other words

s_{i}^{2}(x_{u})=\lambda_{ui},\qquad(\nabla(s_{i}^{2}))(x_{u})=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad(\hess(s_{i}^{2}))(x_{u})=0

for all
i\in\{0,\ldots,m\}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
It suffices to show that there is
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that the polynomial

\ell-s_{0}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}s_{i}^{2}(1-g_{i})^{2k}g_{i}\overset{(***)}{\underset{(**)}{\in}}\bigcap_{u\in F}I_{x_{u}}^{2}

lies in
M
since this implies immediately
\ell\in M
. By Theorem
1.14
, this task reduces to find
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

f_{k}>0
on
S\setminus F
and
(\hess(f_{k}))(u)\succ 0
for all
u\in F
where

f_{k}:=\st(\ell)-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\st(s_{i}^{2})(1-g_{i})^{2k}g_{i}\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]

is the standard part of this polynomial. Note for later use that
f_{k}
and
\nabla f_{k}
vanish on
F
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
In order to find such a
k
, we calculate

\displaystyle(\hess f_{k})(u)

\displaystyle\overset{(***)}{=}-\sum_{i=1}^{m}\st(\lambda_{ui})\hess((1-g_{i})^{2k}g_{i})(u)

\displaystyle\overset{\ref{derexo}}{\underset{(*)}{=}}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\st(\lambda_{ui})(4k(\nabla g_{i})(\nabla g_{i})^{T}-\hess g_{i})(u)

for
u\in F
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Lemma
2.10
we can choose
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

g_{i}(1-g_{i})^{2k}
is strictly concave on
\{x\in F\mid g_{i}(x)=0\}
for
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. Since
\st(\lambda_{1})+\ldots+\st(\lambda_{m})>0

[
\to

3.8
(c)], we get together with
(*)
and
2.9
that for all sufficiently large
k
, we
have
(\hess f_{k})(u)\succ 0
for all
u\in F
. In particular, we can choose
k_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\hess(f_{k_{0}})(u)\succ 0
for all
u\in F
. Since
f_{k_{0}}
and
\nabla f_{k_{0}}
vanish on
F
,
we have by elementary analysis that there is an open subset
U
of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
F
such that
f_{k_{0}}>0
on
U\setminus F
.
Now
S\setminus U
is compact so that we can choose
N\in\mathbb{N}
with
\st(\ell)\geq\frac{1}{N}
and

\st(s_{i}^{2})\leq N
on
S\setminus U
. Then
f_{k}\geq\frac{1}{N}-m\frac{N}{2k}
on
S\setminus U
by
Exercise
4.1
since
0\leq g_{i}\leq 1
on
S
for all
i\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
For all sufficiently large
k\in\mathbb{N}
with
k\geq k_{0}
, we now have
f_{k}>0
on
S\setminus U
and
because of
f_{k}\geq f_{k_{0}}>0
on
(S\cap U)\setminus F
(use again that
0\leq g_{i}\leq 1
on
S
) even
f_{k}>0
on
S\setminus F
.
∎

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
such that
M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is Archimedean and suppose that
S:=S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

has nonempty interior near its convex boundary.
Suppose that
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave on
(\convbd S)\cap Z(g_{i})
for each

i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
. Let
R
be a real closed extension field of
\mathbb{R}
and

\ell\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]_{1}
such that
\ell\geq 0
on
\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S)
.
Then
\ell
lies in the quadratic module generated by
g_{1},\ldots,g_{m}
in
R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
.

Corollary 4.4
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
such that
M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is Archimedean and suppose that
S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

has nonempty interior near its convex boundary.
Suppose that
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave on
(\convbd S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)\cap Z(g_{i})
for each

i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
.
Then there exists

d\in\mathbb{N}

such that for all
\ell\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]_{1}
with
\ell\geq 0
on
S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
, we have

\ell\in M_{d}(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g).

Proof.

(cf. the proofs of Theorems
4.5
and
2.3
)
For each
d\in\mathbb{N}
, consider the class
S_{d}

of all pairs
(R,a_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
where
R
is a real closed extension field of
\mathbb{R}
and

a_{0},a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in R

such that whenever

\forall x\in\transfer_{\mathbb{R},R}(S):a_{1}x_{1}+\ldots+a_{n}x_{n}+a_{0}\geq 0

holds, the polynomial
a_{1}X_{1}+\ldots+a_{n}X_{n}+a_{0}
is a sum
of
d
elements from
R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
where each term in the sum is of degree at most
d

and is of the form
p^{2}g_{i}
with
p\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
and
i\in\{0,\dots,m\}
where
g_{0}:=1\in R[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]
[
\to

2.8
(a)].
By real quantifier elimination
8.17
, it
is easy to see that this is an
(n+1)
-ary
\mathbb{R}
-semialgebraic class.
Set
\mathscr{E}:=\{S_{d}\mid d\in\mathbb{N}\}
and
observe that
\forall d_{1},d_{2}\in\mathbb{N}:\exists d_{3}\in\mathbb{N}:S_{d_{1}}\cup S_{d_{2}}\subseteq S_{d_{3}}
(take
d_{3}:=\max\{d_{1},d_{2}\}
). By
4.3
,
we have
\bigcup\mathscr{E}=\mathscr{R}_{n+1}
. Now

4.2
yields
\set_{\mathbb{R}}(S_{d})=\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
for some
d\in\mathbb{N}
.
∎

Our lecture notes culminate in the following result which is a contribution to the theory of solving systems of polynomial inequalities.

Corollary 4.5

(Kriel, Schweighofer
[
KS’
]
)
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]^{m}
such that
M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is Archimedean and suppose that
S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

has nonempty interior near its convex boundary.
Suppose that
g_{i}
is strictly quasiconcave on
(\convbd S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g))\cap Z(g_{i})
for each

i\in\{1,\dots,m\}
. Then

S_{d}(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)=\conv S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)

for all sufficiently large
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

First consider the special case
S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)=\emptyset
. In this case,
-1\in M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
for example by
2.14
or by
4.4
. By Definition
1.5
, this
entails
L_{d}(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)=\emptyset
and thus
S_{d}(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)=\emptyset=S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
for all sufficiently large
d\in\mathbb{N}
.

Now suppose that
S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)\neq\emptyset
. Since
M(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is Archimedean,
S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)
is then compact. By
3.5
and
1.19
, it follows that
\convbd S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)\neq\emptyset
.
In particular,
S^{\circ}\neq\emptyset
by Definition
3.4
. Hence the conditions of Theorem
1.16
are met and what we have to show is therefore
exactly that there is
d\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\forall f\in\mathbb{R}[\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to7.25557pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to5.6817pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.7584pt{}}}$\hss}}}X]_{1}:(f\geq 0\text{ on }S(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)\implies f\in M_{d}(\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to4.10278pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to3.32196pt{}}}$\hss}}}{\hbox to0.0pt{{$\underline{{\hbox to2.94585pt{}}}$\hss}}}g)).

But this is exactly what Corollary
4.4
says.
∎
